# Authors of every work cited in the MAI-Thinking-1 technical report

**Source report:** *MAI-Thinking-1: Building a Hill-Climbing Machine* — The Microsoft AI Team, Microsoft AI, 2 June 2026.  
[Report PDF](https://microsoft.ai/pdf/mai-thinking-1.pdf) · [Announcement post](https://microsoft.ai/news/introducing-mai-thinking-1/)

The report carries no individual byline — it is credited collectively to "The Microsoft AI Team", so there is no author list for the report itself to map.

| | |
|---|---|
| References in the bibliography | **202** |
| Unique authors across them | **5362** |
| Authors with a published email | **151** (2.8%) |
| Authors appearing on more than one cited work | **924** |

## Method — and what "verified" means here

1. **Bibliography extracted from the report PDF itself** (pages 61–81), not from memory or a summary. Entry boundaries were recovered from the hanging-indent x-positions so no reference was merged or split.
2. **Author lists were re-fetched from the source of record for each work**, rather than trusted from the report's own bibliography. Where the report abbreviates with *et al.*, the full list comes from the source:

| Author-list source | References |
|---|---|
| arXiv API | 126 |
| DBLP | 42 |
| as credited on the artifact / report bibliography | 24 |
| ACL Anthology bibtex dump | 9 |
| OpenAI blog post byline | 1 |

3. **Every resolved list was cross-checked against the printed citation** (first author present, ≥60% name overlap) to catch a lookup landing on the wrong paper. **0 of 202 failed this check.**
4. **Emails are only reported where the paper itself prints them** — harvested from the first two pages of each paper PDF and matched to an author by name. Nothing is inferred from name + affiliation, and nothing is guessed. **A blank email means "not published in the paper," not "no email exists."** Coverage is low (2.8%) because most papers print emails for only the corresponding authors, and several of the largest author lists (e.g. the DeepSeek reports) print none at all.
5. **Non-paper references are kept and labelled** — GitHub repos, blog posts, model cards, books, and specs. For those the "author" is whoever is credited on the artifact, which is often an organization.

### Known limitations

- Author names are recorded as each source of record spells them; the same person under two spellings across two papers will appear as two rows. Names were normalized (case, accents, punctuation) before deduplication, but no identity resolution beyond that was attempted.
- Common Chinese-transliterated names (e.g. multiple distinct researchers named "Wei Wang") are **not** disambiguated. A row with many papers may conflate more than one person.
- R23 is an OpenAI blog post; `openai.com` returns HTTP 403 to automated fetches, so its byline was read from a verbatim third-party reproduction rather than the original page.
- Emails were harvested only from arXiv-hosted PDFs, so papers cited only via a publisher or proceedings link contribute no emails even if the published version prints them.
